=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer mountainHeight denoting the height of a mountain.
You are also given an integer array workerTimes representing the work time of workers in seconds.
The workers work simultaneously to reduce the height of the mountain. For worker i:

To decrease the mountain's height by x, it takes workerTimes[i] + workerTimes[i] * 2 + ... + workerTimes[i] * x seconds. For example:

	
To reduce the height of the mountain by 1, it takes workerTimes[i] seconds.
To reduce the height of the mountain by 2, it takes workerTimes[i] + workerTimes[i] * 2 seconds, and so on.



Return an integer representing the minimum number of seconds required for the workers to make the height of the mountain 0.
 
Example 1:

Input: mountainHeight = 4, workerTimes = [2,1,1]
Output: 3
Explanation:
One way the height of the mountain can be reduced to 0 is:

Worker 0 reduces the height by 1, taking workerTimes[0] = 2 seconds.
Worker 1 reduces the height by 2, taking workerTimes[1] + workerTimes[1] * 2 = 3 seconds.
Worker 2 reduces the height by 1, taking workerTimes[2] = 1 second.

Since they work simultaneously, the minimum time needed is max(2, 3, 1) = 3 seconds.

Example 2:

Input: mountainHeight = 10, workerTimes = [3,2,2,4]
Output: 12
Explanation:

Worker 0 reduces the height by 2, taking workerTimes[0] + workerTimes[0] * 2 = 9 seconds.
Worker 1 reduces the height by 3, taking workerTimes[1] + workerTimes[1] * 2 + workerTimes[1] * 3 = 12 seconds.
Worker 2 reduces the height by 3, taking workerTimes[2] + workerTimes[2] * 2 + workerTimes[2] * 3 = 12 seconds.
Worker 3 reduces the height by 2, taking workerTimes[3] + workerTimes[3] * 2 = 12 seconds.

The number of seconds needed is max(9, 12, 12, 12) = 12 seconds.

Example 3:

Input: mountainHeight = 5, workerTimes = [1]
Output: 15
Explanation:
There is only one worker in this example, so the answer is workerTimes[0] + workerTimes[0] * 2 + workerTimes[0] * 3 + workerTimes[0] * 4 + workerTimes[0] * 5 = 15.

 
Constraints:

1 <= mountainHeight <= 10^5
1 <= workerTimes.length <= 10^4
1 <= workerTimes[i] <= 10^6

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minNumberOfSeconds(self, mountainHeight: int, workerTimes: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

total=0

    if total >= mountainHeight: return True

Need maybe include `List` import. The final code:

```python
from typing import List
from math import isqrt

class Solution:
    def minNumberOfSeconds(self, mountainHeight: int, workerTimes: List[int]) -> int:
        def can(time: int) -> bool:
            total = 0
            for w in workerTimes:
                # max x with w*x*(x+1)//2 <= time
                a = (2 * time) // w
                x = (isqrt(1 + 4 * a) - 1) // 2
                if x > mountainHeight:
                    x = mountainHeight
                total += x
                if total >= mountainHeight:
                    return True
            return False

        lo = 0
        hi = min(workerTimes) * mountainHeight * (mountainHeight + 1) // 2
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if can(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        return lo
```

Roots: [-1 ± sqrt(1+4a)]/2. Positive root r = (-1+sqrt(1+4a))/2. Largest integer x <= r is floor(r). Let s=isqrt(1+4a). Need show floor((s-1)/2) = floor((sqrt(1+4a)-1)/2). We argued. Let's prove:
Let N=1+4a. s=floor(sqrt N). Then s <= sqrt N < s+1.
If s is even, s=2k. Then (s-1)/2 = k - 0.5, s/2=k. sqrt N < 2k+1 => (sqrt N -1)/2 < k. sqrt N >= 2k => (sqrt N -1)/2 >= k - 0.5. So floor = k-1 = (s-1)//2. If s is odd, s=2k+1. Then (s-1)/2=k, s/2=k+0.5. sqrt N >=2k+1 => (sqrt N -1)/2 >= k. sqrt N <2k+2 => (sqrt N -1)/2 < k+0.5. floor = k = (s-1)//2. Good. So formula exact.

```
from typing import List
from math import isqrt

class Solution:
    def minNumberOfSeconds(self, mountainHeight: int, workerTimes: List[int]) -> int:
        def can(t: int) -> bool:
            total = 0
            for w in workerTimes:
                # w * x * (x + 1) / 2 <= t
                a = (2 * t) // w
                x = (isqrt(1 + 4 * a) - 1) // 2
                if x > mountainHeight:
                    x = mountainHeight
                total += x
                if total >= mountainHeight:
                    return True
            return False

        lo, hi = 0, min(workerTimes) * mountainHeight * (mountainHeight + 1) // 2
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if can(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        return lo
```
This is good.

Now, maybe if there is a possible issue with "workerTimes" mixed, e.g., H=100000, n=10000, one worker w=1, rest w=1e6. What is optimum? Fast worker can do many units, slow workers maybe some. Binary search works. high=min_w*5e9=5e9. can(mid) for mid maybe around? Fast worker x ~
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from math import isqrt

class Solution:
    def minNumberOfSeconds(self, mountainHeight: int, workerTimes: List[int]) -> int:
        def can(time: int) -> bool:
            total = 0
            for w in workerTimes:
                # Max x such that w * x * (x + 1) / 2 <= time
                a = (2 * time) // w
                x = (isqrt(1 + 4 * a) - 1) // 2
                if x > mountainHeight:
                    x = mountainHeight
                total += x
                if total >= mountainHeight:
                    return True
            return False

        lo = 0
        hi = min(workerTimes) * mountainHeight * (mountainHeight + 1) // 2

        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if can(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid + 1

        return lo
```
